# Performance baseline run 2026-09-19

Generated by `scripts/perf/report.py` from the JSON in this directory. The analysis that
reads these numbers is `docs/perf/BASELINE-<date>.md`; this file is the evidence.

## Environment

- date: `2026-09-19`
- git_sha: `842daf33ce7e02b50d6c95fba1321667a92d71c0`
- git_branch: `claude/app-performance-baseline-74jqdt`
- rustc: `rustc 1.94.1 (e408947bf 2026-03-25)`
- profile: `dev (opt-level 1 workspace, 3 dependencies; the profile run.sh launches)`
- kernel: `6.18.44-fc-v37`
- cpu: `Intel(R) Xeon(R) Processor @ 2.80GHz`
- cores: `4`
- mem_gib: `15.7`
- valgrind: `valgrind-3.22.0`
- strace: `strace -- version 6.8`
- load_at_start: `[0.34375, 0.8681640625, 0.90771484375]`
- container: `False`

## How to read the tables

- **p50 / p95**: wall time of one iteration of the measured region, in process, no socket unless the name says so.
- **cpu**: process CPU time per iteration (user + system, every thread). Above wall means other threads worked; below means the iteration waited.
- **allocs / bytes**: heap allocations and bytes requested per iteration, counted by a global allocator wrapper.
- **syscalls**: from a separate strace pass with fewer iterations; the count is per iteration, every thread.
- **Ir**: instructions retired per iteration under callgrind, deterministic. The breakdown is self cost by subsystem.
- **result**: size of the JSON result the op returned, which is what crosses the socket to a client.
- A row whose outcome is an error code measured that refusal path; the note beside the group says why.

## Ten times the rows

## Where the instructions go

Per scenario, under callgrind: instructions per iteration, self cost by subsystem (every bucket at or above 0.5 %), the inclusive share of a few known entry points, and the Relay functions with the largest inclusive cost below the pipeline. Inclusive figures overlap; they answer "how much of this op is X".

## Memory over 20 000 mixed operations

Ten ops per round (session.list, task.list, a keystroke, a scrollback tail, task.update, notes.list, settings.set, mailbox.list, app.status, git.status). Live heap is what the allocator wrapper has handed out and not been given back; RSS is the process; store is the SQLite file.

| ops | live heap | RSS | store | threads |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 2.6 MiB | 23.6 MiB | 0.6 MiB | 7 |
| 2000 | 2.7 MiB | 25.4 MiB | 0.9 MiB | 8 |
| 4000 | 2.7 MiB | 26.0 MiB | 1.3 MiB | 8 |
| 6000 | 2.7 MiB | 26.7 MiB | 1.7 MiB | 8 |
| 8000 | 2.7 MiB | 27.3 MiB | 2.1 MiB | 8 |
| 10000 | 2.7 MiB | 28.0 MiB | 2.4 MiB | 8 |
| 12000 | 2.8 MiB | 28.6 MiB | 2.8 MiB | 8 |
| 14000 | 2.8 MiB | 29.3 MiB | 3.2 MiB | 8 |
| 16000 | 2.8 MiB | 29.8 MiB | 3.6 MiB | 8 |
| 18000 | 2.8 MiB | 30.6 MiB | 3.9 MiB | 8 |
| 20000 | 2.8 MiB | 31.1 MiB | 4.4 MiB | 8 |

## The engine process from the outside

A real `relay serve` (dev profile) with six fake sessions, sampled from `/proc` over 30 s windows. Wakeups are voluntary context switches summed over every thread; CPU is at kernel tick resolution.

- startup on a fresh store: **0.028 s** to the first answered ping; RSS 15.7 MiB fresh, 22.0 MiB with six sessions
- restart with six live sessions to reap: **1.508 s**; clean restarts: 0.037 s, 0.032 s

| phase | CPU % | CPU ms in window | wakeups/s | preemptions/s | RSS | threads |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| idle six quiet sessions | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 22.0 MiB | 17 |
| idle resources watch on | 0.033 | 10.0 | 1.2 | 0.0 | 22.2 MiB | 17 |
| output 60 lines per s unattached | 0.133 | 40.0 | 66.7 | 1.4 | 22.3 MiB | 17 |
| output 60 lines per s attached | 0.533 | 160.0 | 177.5 | 8.3 | 23.0 MiB | 17 |

- burst of 50 000 lines (4.9 MB) to an attached client: stream quiet after **3.976 s** (includes a 1 s silence wait), 140.0 ms engine CPU, 2414 frames, 1.70 MiB on the wire carrying 1.14 MiB of the 4.67 MiB payload

| CLI call | min | p50 | max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `relay ping` | 3.58 ms | 3.82 ms | 4.13 ms |
| `relay q session list` | 4.06 ms | 4.44 ms | 4.95 ms |
| `relay q app status` | 3.93 ms | 4.27 ms | 4.63 ms |
